- **hunt-agent-auth-bypass**: Authorization bypass in AI-agent tools — role/persona claims, unverifiable named-credential exceptions, privilege fields inside attacker-supplied credential documents, privilege escalation via prompt injection, data-classification override, and 'I-am-the-admin' attacks against prompt-level access control. - **hunt-agent-browser-use**: Attacks specific to computer-use and browser agents — agents that see a screen/DOM and act by clicking, typing, and navigating (AI browsers like Comet/Atlas, ChatGPT Operator, Claude Computer-Use, RPA copilots). - **hunt-agent-brute-force**: Brute-force and rate-limiting weaknesses in AI-agent tools — PIN/token/password guessing with no lockout, and feedback oracles (correct-digit / too-high-low / Mastermind-style) that collapse the search space. Use when a tool accepts guesses and returns feedback without throttling. ([elementalsouls/Claude-AgentHunter](https://github.com/elementalsouls/Claude-AgentHunter/tree/HEAD/skills/hunt-agent-brute-force) · ★29)

- **hunt-agent-crypto**: Cryptographic weaknesses in AI-agent tools that encrypt/decrypt/sign or mint tokens — ECB cut-and-paste and byte-at-a-time, padding oracles, missing MAC, hardcoded keys/predictable IVs. - **hunt-agent-deserialization**: Unsafe deserialization in AI-agent tools that parse structured data — YAML, pickle, JSON, XML, TOML — via tool parameters, AND poisoned model/dataset artifacts loaded from a hub (Hugging Face / registry pickle: torch.load, .pkl/.pt/.ckpt). - **hunt-http-smuggling**: Hunt HTTP request smuggling (CL.TE, TE.CL, H2.CL, H2.TE). Cause: front-end proxy and back-end server disagree on where one request ends and the next begins (Content-Length vs Transfer-Encoding header parsing inconsistency). CL.TE: front-end uses CL, back uses TE → smuggle by sending TE: chunked but with body that fits CL count. TE.CL: opposite. H2.CL: HTTP/2 downgrade, smuggle CL into HTTP/1.1 back-end. Detection tools: Burp HTTP Request Smuggler extension, smuggler.py, h2csmuggler.
